{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_01FY4zLYAUvVj1t8GaFY2sYx","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01Ludh5fStmZrAPnYiAAnH46","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/app/workspace/environment/skills/erlang-ssh-protocol.md","content":"# Erlang SSH Protocol Implementation\n\n## Overview\nThe Erlang/OTP SSH library implements the SSH protocol as defined in RFC 4253 and related RFCs. Understanding the SSH protocol message flow is critical for security analysis.\n\n## SSH Protocol Message Flow\n\n### Connection Establishment\n1. **Version Exchange**: Client and server exchange version strings\n2. **Key Exchange (KEX)**: Algorithm negotiation and key generation\n3. **Authentication**: User authentication phase\n4. **Channel Operations**: After authentication, channels can be opened\n\n### Message Types\nSSH protocol messages are identified by a message type byte:\n\n```erlang\n% Transport Layer (RFC 4253)\n-define(SSH_MSG_DISCONNECT, 1).\n-define(SSH_MSG_IGNORE, 2).\n-define(SSH_MSG_UNIMPLEMENTED, 3).\n-define(SSH_MSG_DEBUG, 4).\n-define(SSH_MSG_SERVICE_REQUEST, 5).\n-define(SSH_MSG_SERVICE_ACCEPT, 6).\n\n% Key Exchange Messages (20-49)\n-define(SSH_MSG_KEXINIT, 20).\n-define(SSH_MSG_NEWKEYS, 21).\n\n% User Authentication (50-79)\n-define(SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_REQUEST, 50).\n-define(SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_FAILURE, 51).\n-define(SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_SUCCESS, 52).\n\n% Connection Protocol (80-127)\n-define(SSH_MSG_GLOBAL_REQUEST, 80).\n-define(SSH_MSG_REQUEST_SUCCESS, 81).\n-define(SSH_MSG_REQUEST_FAILURE, 82).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN, 90).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN_CONFIRMATION, 91).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN_FAILURE, 92).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_WINDOW_ADJUST, 93).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_DATA, 94).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_EXTENDED_DATA, 95).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_EOF, 96).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_CLOSE, 97).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, 98).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_SUCCESS, 99).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE, 100).\n```\n\n## Security Considerations\n\n### Authentication State\nMessages that can execute commands (like `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST` with \"exec\" type) should ONLY be processed after successful authentication.\n\n### Critical Security Check\n```erlang\n% Before processing channel requests, verify:\n% 1. Authentication is complete\n% 2. User has been authenticated\n% 3. Channel is properly established\n```\n\n### Common Vulnerabilities\n1. **Pre-authentication command execution**: Processing channel or exec requests before authentication\n2. **State confusion**: Not properly tracking connection state (pre-auth vs post-auth)\n3. **Message ordering**: Accepting out-of-order messages that bypass security checks\n\n## Erlang/OTP SSH Implementation\n\n### Key Files\n- `ssh_connection_handler.erl`: Main state machine for SSH connections\n- `ssh_connection.erl`: Channel and connection protocol handling\n- `ssh_auth.erl`: Authentication logic\n- `ssh_message.erl`: Message encoding/decoding\n\n### State Machine\nThe SSH connection handler typically uses a gen_statem behavior with states like:\n- `{hello, ...}`: Initial version exchange\n- `{kexinit, ...}`: Key exchange\n- `{service_request, ...}`: Service negotiation\n- `{userauth, ...}`: Authentication phase\n- `{connected, ...}`: Authenticated, normal operations\n\n### Vulnerability Pattern\nA common vulnerability occurs when message handlers don't verify the current state before executing privileged operations.\n\n```erlang\n% VULNERABLE - processes exec without checking auth state\nhandle_message(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data, State) ->\n    % Parse and execute command - NO AUTH CHECK!\n    execute_command(Data, State).\n\n% SECURE - verifies authentication before processing\nhandle_message(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data, #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    execute_command(Data, State);\nhandle_message(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, _Data, State) ->\n    % Reject if not authenticated\n    {disconnect, ?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR, State}.\n```\n\n## References\n- RFC 4253: SSH Transport Layer Protocol\n- RFC 4254: SSH Connection Protocol\n- RFC 4252: SSH Authentication Protocol\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":104,"cache_read_input_tokens":19284,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":104,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"67a03150-1814-4744-9310-d4e4323cf882","uuid":"42dcd8ac-f2cf-4460-a558-b0db0ece5a68"}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_01FY4zLYAUvVj1t8GaFY2sYx","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01BuD1fiyUCVaVuisG7QPp73","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/app/workspace/environment/skills/erlang-pattern-matching.md","content":"# Erlang Pattern Matching and Function Clauses\n\n## Overview\nErlang uses pattern matching extensively for function dispatch, data extraction, and control flow. Understanding pattern matching is essential for both finding and fixing bugs in Erlang code.\n\n## Function Clauses\n\n### Multiple Function Clauses\nFunctions in Erlang can have multiple clauses that are tried in order:\n\n```erlang\n% Function with multiple clauses\nprocess_message(Type, Data, #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    % This clause only matches if authenticated = true\n    handle_authenticated_message(Type, Data, State);\n\nprocess_message(Type, Data, State) ->\n    % This clause is a catch-all\n    handle_unauthenticated_message(Type, Data, State).\n```\n\n### Order Matters\nClauses are evaluated top-to-bottom. More specific patterns should come first:\n\n```erlang\n% CORRECT - specific before general\nfoo([], Acc) -> Acc;\nfoo([H|T], Acc) -> foo(T, [H|Acc]).\n\n% WRONG - general pattern catches everything\nfoo([H|T], Acc) -> foo(T, [H|Acc]);\nfoo([], Acc) -> Acc.  % Never reached!\n```\n\n## Pattern Matching in Record Fields\n\n### Matching Specific Values\n```erlang\n% Only match when authenticated field is true\nhandle(Msg, #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    process_privileged(Msg, State);\n\n% Only match when authenticated is false or undefined\nhandle(Msg, #state{authenticated = false} = State) ->\n    reject(Msg, State).\n```\n\n### Guards\nAdditional conditions can be added with guards:\n\n```erlang\n% Match when authenticated AND user is admin\nhandle(Msg, #state{authenticated = true, role = admin} = State)\n    when is_binary(Msg) ->\n    process_admin_command(Msg, State);\n\n% Match authenticated non-admin users\nhandle(Msg, #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    process_user_command(Msg, State).\n```\n\n## Security Implications\n\n### Weak Pattern Matching\n```erlang\n% VULNERABLE - catches all states\nhandle_exec_request(Cmd, State) ->\n    execute(Cmd).  % No authentication check!\n```\n\n### Strong Pattern Matching\n```erlang\n% SECURE - only matches authenticated states\nhandle_exec_request(Cmd, #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    execute(Cmd, State);\n\nhandle_exec_request(_Cmd, State) ->\n    {error, not_authenticated}.\n```\n\n## State Machines and Pattern Matching\n\nIn gen_statem, pattern matching on state name provides security:\n\n```erlang\n% Only allow channel requests in connected state\nhandle_event(internal, {channel_request, Req}, {connected, _}, Data) ->\n    process_channel_request(Req, Data);\n\n% Reject channel requests in any other state\nhandle_event(internal, {channel_request, _}, _State, _Data) ->\n    {keep_state_and_data, [{reply, error}]}.\n```\n\n## Common Patterns for Security\n\n### Whitelist Approach\n```erlang\n% Only allow specific messages before authentication\nhandle_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_KEXINIT, Data, State) -> process_kex(Data, State);\nhandle_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_NEWKEYS, Data, State) -> process_newkeys(Data, State);\nhandle_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_REQUEST, Data, State) -> process_auth(Data, State);\nhandle_pre_auth(_, _, State) -> {disconnect, protocol_error, State}.\n```\n\n### State-Based Dispatch\n```erlang\n% Dispatch based on authentication state\ndispatch(Msg, #state{auth_complete = false} = State) ->\n    handle_pre_auth(Msg, State);\n\ndispatch(Msg, #state{auth_complete = true} = State) ->\n    handle_post_auth(Msg, State).\n```\n\n## Finding Vulnerabilities\n\nLook for:\n1. Functions that don't check authentication state\n2. Catch-all patterns that allow unauthorized operations\n3. Missing guards on privileged operations\n4. State transitions that bypass authentication\n\n## Example Fix Pattern\n```erlang\n% BEFORE (vulnerable)\nhandle_message(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data, State) ->\n    process_request(Data, State).\n\n% AFTER (secure)\nhandle_message(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data,\n               #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    process_request(Data, State);\nhandle_message(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, _Data, State) ->\n    disconnect(protocol_error, State).\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":104,"cache_read_input_tokens":19284,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":104,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"67a03150-1814-4744-9310-d4e4323cf882","uuid":"272b4ba1-b340-4a04-b75e-14d82bd22925"}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_01FY4zLYAUvVj1t8GaFY2sYx","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01EMDenyjkmHo9j4c7vKspBv","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/app/workspace/environment/skills/ssh-authentication-flow.md","content":"# SSH Authentication Flow and Security\n\n## Overview\nSSH authentication must complete successfully before any privileged operations (like command execution) are allowed. Understanding this flow is critical for identifying authentication bypass vulnerabilities.\n\n## Standard SSH Connection Flow\n\n### Phase 1: Transport Layer Setup\n1. **TCP Connection**: Client connects to server on port 22\n2. **Version Exchange**:\n   - Server sends: `SSH-2.0-ServerVersion`\n   - Client sends: `SSH-2.0-ClientVersion`\n3. **Key Exchange (KEX)**:\n   - Client sends: `SSH_MSG_KEXINIT`\n   - Server sends: `SSH_MSG_KEXINIT`\n   - Exchange continues with algorithm-specific messages\n   - Both sides send: `SSH_MSG_NEWKEYS`\n\n### Phase 2: Service Request\n4. **Service Request**:\n   - Client sends: `SSH_MSG_SERVICE_REQUEST` (service: \"ssh-userauth\")\n   - Server sends: `SSH_MSG_SERVICE_ACCEPT`\n\n### Phase 3: Authentication\n5. **User Authentication**:\n   - Client sends: `SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_REQUEST` (with credentials)\n   - Server validates credentials\n   - Server sends: `SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_SUCCESS` or `SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_FAILURE`\n\n### Phase 4: Connection Protocol (POST-AUTHENTICATION ONLY)\n6. **Service Request** (if needed):\n   - Client sends: `SSH_MSG_SERVICE_REQUEST` (service: \"ssh-connection\")\n   - Server sends: `SSH_MSG_SERVICE_ACCEPT`\n\n7. **Channel Operations**:\n   - Client sends: `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN`\n   - Server sends: `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN_CONFIRMATION`\n   - Client sends: `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST` (type: \"exec\", \"shell\", \"subsystem\")\n   - Command execution begins\n\n## Security Boundary\n\n### PRE-AUTHENTICATION Messages (Safe)\nThese messages MUST be allowed before authentication:\n- `SSH_MSG_DISCONNECT` (1)\n- `SSH_MSG_IGNORE` (2)\n- `SSH_MSG_DEBUG` (4)\n- `SSH_MSG_SERVICE_REQUEST` (5) - only for \"ssh-userauth\"\n- `SSH_MSG_KEXINIT` (20)\n- `SSH_MSG_NEWKEYS` (21)\n- KEX-specific messages (30-49)\n- `SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_REQUEST` (50)\n\n### POST-AUTHENTICATION Messages (Dangerous)\nThese messages MUST be blocked before authentication:\n- `SSH_MSG_GLOBAL_REQUEST` (80)\n- `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN` (90)\n- `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST` (98) - **CRITICAL: Can execute commands**\n- `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_DATA` (94)\n\n## Common Vulnerability: Pre-Auth Channel Requests\n\n### The Attack\nAn attacker sends `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST` with type \"exec\" BEFORE completing authentication:\n\n```\n1. TCP Connect\n2. Version Exchange\n3. ATTACKER sends: SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST with \"exec\" and command\n4. If server doesn't check auth state, command executes!\n```\n\n### Vulnerable Code Pattern\n```erlang\n% VULNERABLE - no auth check before processing channel request\nhandle_msg(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST,\n           <<ChannelId:32, \"exec\", 1, CmdLen:32, Cmd:CmdLen/binary, _/binary>>,\n           State) ->\n    % Executes command without checking if user is authenticated!\n    execute_command(Cmd, State).\n```\n\n### Secure Code Pattern\n```erlang\n% SECURE - verify authentication state first\nhandle_msg(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data,\n           #state{authenticated = true, channels = Channels} = State) ->\n    % Only process if authenticated AND channel exists\n    process_channel_request(Data, State);\n\nhandle_msg(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, _Data,\n           #state{authenticated = false} = State) ->\n    % Disconnect if not authenticated\n    {disconnect, ?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR, State}.\n```\n\n## State Machine Security\n\n### Proper State Tracking\n```erlang\n-record(state, {\n    connection_state,  % {hello} | {kexinit} | {service_request} | {userauth} | {connected}\n    authenticated,     % true | false\n    user,             % authenticated username\n    channels          % active channels (only after auth)\n}).\n```\n\n### State-Based Message Filtering\n```erlang\n% In pre-auth states, only allow specific messages\nhandle_event({hello, _}, ?SSH_MSG_KEXINIT, Data, State) ->\n    {next_state, {kexinit, server}, process_kex(Data, State)};\n\nhandle_event({hello, _}, InvalidMsg, _Data, State) ->\n    {stop, {protocol_error, invalid_message_in_hello}, State}.\n\n% In connected state, allow channel operations\nhandle_event({connected, _}, ?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data, State) ->\n    process_channel_request(Data, State).\n```\n\n## Fixing Authentication Bypass Vulnerabilities\n\n### Step 1: Identify Message Handlers\nFind all functions that handle SSH messages, especially:\n- Channel open\n- Channel request (exec, shell, subsystem)\n- Global requests\n\n### Step 2: Add Authentication Checks\nEnsure each handler verifies authentication state:\n\n```erlang\n% Pattern 1: Match on authenticated state\nhandle(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data, #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    process(Data, State);\nhandle(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, _, State) ->\n    {disconnect, not_authenticated, State}.\n\n% Pattern 2: Guard clause\nhandle(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data, State)\n    when State#state.authenticated =:= true ->\n    process(Data, State);\nhandle(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, _, State) ->\n    {disconnect, not_authenticated, State}.\n\n% Pattern 3: Explicit check\nhandle(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data, State) ->\n    case State#state.authenticated of\n        true -> process(Data, State);\n        false -> {disconnect, not_authenticated, State}\n    end.\n```\n\n### Step 3: Test Edge Cases\n- Message sent before version exchange\n- Message sent during key exchange\n- Message sent before authentication\n- Message sent after failed authentication\n- Message sent after successful authentication (should work)\n\n## References\n- RFC 4252: SSH Authentication Protocol\n- RFC 4254: SSH Connection Protocol\n- CVE patterns: Pre-authentication command Understanding the SSH protocol message flow is critical for security analysis.\n\n## SSH Protocol Message Flow\n\n### Connection Establishment\n1. **Version Exchange**: Client and server exchange version strings\n2. **Key Exchange (KEX)**: Algorithm negotiation and key generation\n3. **Authentication**: User authentication phase\n4. **Channel Operations**: After authentication, channels can be opened\n\n### Message Types\nSSH protocol messages are identified by a message type byte:\n\n```erlang\n% Transport Layer (RFC 4253)\n-define(SSH_MSG_DISCONNECT, 1).\n-define(SSH_MSG_IGNORE, 2).\n-define(SSH_MSG_UNIMPLEMENTED, 3).\n-define(SSH_MSG_DEBUG, 4).\n-define(SSH_MSG_SERVICE_REQUEST, 5).\n-define(SSH_MSG_SERVICE_ACCEPT, 6).\n\n% Key Exchange Messages (20-49)\n-define(SSH_MSG_KEXINIT, 20).\n-define(SSH_MSG_NEWKEYS, 21).\n\n% User Authentication (50-79)\n-define(SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_REQUEST, 50).\n-define(SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_FAILURE, 51).\n-define(SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_SUCCESS, 52).\n\n% Connection Protocol (80-127)\n-define(SSH_MSG_GLOBAL_REQUEST, 80).\n-define(SSH_MSG_REQUEST_SUCCESS, 81).\n-define(SSH_MSG_REQUEST_FAILURE, 82).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN, 90).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN_CONFIRMATION, 91).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN_FAILURE, 92).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_WINDOW_ADJUST, 93).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_DATA, 94).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_EXTENDED_DATA, 95).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_EOF, 96).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_CLOSE, 97).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, 98).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_SUCCESS, 99).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE, 100).\n```\n\n## Security Considerations\n\n### Authentication State\nMessages that can execute commands (like `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST` with \"exec\" type) should ONLY be processed after successful authentication.\n\n### Critical Security Check\n```erlang\n% Before processing channel requests, verify:\n% 1. Authentication is complete\n% 2. User has been authenticated\n% 3. Channel is properly established\n```\n\n### Common Vulnerabilities\n1. **Pre-authentication command execution**: Processing channel or exec requests before authentication\n2. **State confusion**: Not properly tracking connection state (pre-auth vs post-auth)\n3. **Message ordering**: Accepting out-of-order messages that bypass security checks\n\n## Erlang/OTP SSH Implementation\n\n### Key Files\n- `ssh_connection_handler.erl`: Main state machine for SSH connections\n- `ssh_connection.erl`: Channel and connection protocol handling\n- `ssh_auth.erl`: Authentication logic\n- `ssh_message.erl`: Message encoding/decoding\n\n### State Machine\nThe SSH connection handler typically uses a gen_statem behavior with states like:\n- `{hello, ...}`: Initial version exchange\n- `{kexinit, ...}`: Key exchange\n- `{service_request, ...}`: Service negotiation\n- `{userauth, ...}`: Authentication phase\n- `{connected, ...}`: Authenticated, normal operations\n\n### Vulnerability Pattern\nA common vulnerability occurs when message handlers don't verify the current state before executing privileged operations.\n\n```erlang\n% VULNERABLE - processes exec without checking auth state\nhandle_message(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data, State) ->\n    % Parse and execute command - NO AUTH CHECK!\n    execute_command(Data, State).\n\n% SECURE - verifies authentication before processing\nhandle_message(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data, #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    execute_command(Data, State);\nhandle_message(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, _Data, State) ->\n    % Reject if not authenticated\n    {disconnect, ?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR, State}.\n```\n\n## References\n- RFC 4253: SSH Transport Layer Protocol\n- RFC 4254: SSH Connection Protocol\n- RFC 4252: SSH Authentication Protocol\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Understanding pattern matching is essential for both finding and fixing bugs in Erlang code.\n\n## Function Clauses\n\n### Multiple Function Clauses\nFunctions in Erlang can have multiple clauses that are tried in order:\n\n```erlang\n% Function with multiple clauses\nprocess_message(Type, Data, #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    % This clause only matches if authenticated = true\n    handle_authenticated_message(Type, Data, State);\n\nprocess_message(Type, Data, State) ->\n    % This clause is a catch-all\n    handle_unauthenticated_message(Type, Data, State).\n```\n\n### Order Matters\nClauses are evaluated top-to-bottom. More specific patterns should come first:\n\n```erlang\n% CORRECT - specific before general\nfoo([], Acc) -> Acc;\nfoo([H|T], Acc) -> foo(T, [H|Acc]).\n\n% WRONG - general pattern catches everything\nfoo([H|T], Acc) -> foo(T, [H|Acc]);\nfoo([], Acc) -> Acc.  % Never reached!\n```\n\n## Pattern Matching in Record Fields\n\n### Matching Specific Values\n```erlang\n% Only match when authenticated field is true\nhandle(Msg, #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    process_privileged(Msg, State);\n\n% Only match when authenticated is false or undefined\nhandle(Msg, #state{authenticated = false} = State) ->\n    reject(Msg, State).\n```\n\n### Guards\nAdditional conditions can be added with guards:\n\n```erlang\n% Match when authenticated AND user is admin\nhandle(Msg, #state{authenticated = true, role = admin} = State)\n    when is_binary(Msg) ->\n    process_admin_command(Msg, State);\n\n% Match authenticated non-admin users\nhandle(Msg, #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    process_user_command(Msg, State).\n```\n\n## Security Implications\n\n### Weak Pattern Matching\n```erlang\n% VULNERABLE - catches all states\nhandle_exec_request(Cmd, State) ->\n    execute(Cmd).  % No authentication check!\n```\n\n### Strong Pattern Matching\n```erlang\n% SECURE - only matches authenticated states\nhandle_exec_request(Cmd, #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    execute(Cmd, State);\n\nhandle_exec_request(_Cmd, State) ->\n    {error, not_authenticated}.\n```\n\n## State Machines and Pattern Matching\n\nIn gen_statem, pattern matching on state name provides security:\n\n```erlang\n% Only allow channel requests in connected state\nhandle_event(internal, {channel_request, Req}, {connected, _}, Data) ->\n    process_channel_request(Req, Data);\n\n% Reject channel requests in any other state\nhandle_event(internal, {channel_request, _}, _State, _Data) ->\n    {keep_state_and_data, [{reply, error}]}.\n```\n\n## Common Patterns for Security\n\n### Whitelist Approach\n```erlang\n% Only allow specific messages before authentication\nhandle_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_KEXINIT, Data, State) -> process_kex(Data, State);\nhandle_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_NEWKEYS, Data, State) -> process_newkeys(Data, State);\nhandle_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_REQUEST, Data, State) -> process_auth(Data, State);\nhandle_pre_auth(_, _, State) -> {disconnect, protocol_error, State}.\n```\n\n### State-Based Dispatch\n```erlang\n% Dispatch based on authentication state\ndispatch(Msg, #state{auth_complete = false} = State) ->\n    handle_pre_auth(Msg, State);\n\ndispatch(Msg, #state{auth_complete = true} = State) ->\n    handle_post_auth(Msg, State).\n```\n\n## Finding Vulnerabilities\n\nLook for:\n1. Functions that don't check authentication state\n2. Catch-all patterns that allow unauthorized operations\n3. Missing guards on privileged operations\n4. State transitions that bypass authentication\n\n## Example Fix Pattern\n```erlang\n% BEFORE (vulnerable)\nhandle_message(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data, State) ->\n    process_request(Data, State).\n\n% AFTER (secure)\nhandle_message(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data,\n               #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    process_request(Data, State);\nhandle_message(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, _Data, State) ->\n    disconnect(protocol_error, State).\n```\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Understanding this flow is critical for identifying authentication bypass vulnerabilities.\n\n## Standard SSH Connection Flow\n\n### Phase 1: Transport Layer Setup\n1. **TCP Connection**: Client connects to server on port 22\n2. **Version Exchange**:\n   - Server sends: `SSH-2.0-ServerVersion`\n   - Client sends: `SSH-2.0-ClientVersion`\n3. **Key Exchange (KEX)**:\n   - Client sends: `SSH_MSG_KEXINIT`\n   - Server sends: `SSH_MSG_KEXINIT`\n   - Exchange continues with algorithm-specific messages\n   - Both sides send: `SSH_MSG_NEWKEYS`\n\n### Phase 2: Service Request\n4. **Service Request**:\n   - Client sends: `SSH_MSG_SERVICE_REQUEST` (service: \"ssh-userauth\")\n   - Server sends: `SSH_MSG_SERVICE_ACCEPT`\n\n### Phase 3: Authentication\n5. **User Authentication**:\n   - Client sends: `SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_REQUEST` (with credentials)\n   - Server validates credentials\n   - Server sends: `SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_SUCCESS` or `SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_FAILURE`\n\n### Phase 4: Connection Protocol (POST-AUTHENTICATION ONLY)\n6. **Service Request** (if needed):\n   - Client sends: `SSH_MSG_SERVICE_REQUEST` (service: \"ssh-connection\")\n   - Server sends: `SSH_MSG_SERVICE_ACCEPT`\n\n7. **Channel Operations**:\n   - Client sends: `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN`\n   - Server sends: `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN_CONFIRMATION`\n   - Client sends: `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST` (type: \"exec\", \"shell\", \"subsystem\")\n   - Command execution begins\n\n## Security Boundary\n\n### PRE-AUTHENTICATION Messages (Safe)\nThese messages MUST be allowed before authentication:\n- `SSH_MSG_DISCONNECT` (1)\n- `SSH_MSG_IGNORE` (2)\n- `SSH_MSG_DEBUG` (4)\n- `SSH_MSG_SERVICE_REQUEST` (5) - only for \"ssh-userauth\"\n- `SSH_MSG_KEXINIT` (20)\n- `SSH_MSG_NEWKEYS` (21)\n- KEX-specific messages (30-49)\n- `SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_REQUEST` (50)\n\n### POST-AUTHENTICATION Messages (Dangerous)\nThese messages MUST be blocked before authentication:\n- `SSH_MSG_GLOBAL_REQUEST` (80)\n- `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN` (90)\n- `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST` (98) - **CRITICAL: Can execute commands**\n- `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_DATA` (94)\n\n## Common Vulnerability: Pre-Auth Channel Requests\n\n### The Attack\nAn attacker sends `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST` with type \"exec\" BEFORE completing authentication:\n\n```\n1. TCP Connect\n2. Version Exchange\n3. ATTACKER sends: SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST with \"exec\" and command\n4. If server doesn't check auth state, command executes!\n```\n\n### Vulnerable Code Pattern\n```erlang\n% VULNERABLE - no auth check before processing channel request\nhandle_msg(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST,\n           <<ChannelId:32, \"exec\", 1, CmdLen:32, Cmd:CmdLen/binary, _/binary>>,\n           State) ->\n    % Executes command without checking if user is authenticated!\n    execute_command(Cmd, State).\n```\n\n### Secure Code Pattern\n```erlang\n% SECURE - verify authentication state first\nhandle_msg(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data,\n           #state{authenticated = true, channels = Channels} = State) ->\n    % Only process if authenticated AND channel exists\n    process_channel_request(Data, State);\n\nhandle_msg(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, _Data,\n           #state{authenticated = false} = State) ->\n    % Disconnect if not authenticated\n    {disconnect, ?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR, State}.\n```\n\n## State Machine Security\n\n### Proper State Tracking\n```erlang\n-record(state, {\n    connection_state,  % {hello} | {kexinit} | {service_request} | {userauth} | {connected}\n    authenticated,     % true | false\n    user,             % authenticated username\n    channels          % active channels (only after auth)\n}).\n```\n\n### State-Based Message Filtering\n```erlang\n% In pre-auth states, only allow specific messages\nhandle_event({hello, _}, ?SSH_MSG_KEXINIT, Data, State) ->\n    {next_state, {kexinit, server}, process_kex(Data, State)};\n\nhandle_event({hello, _}, InvalidMsg, _Data, State) ->\n    {stop, {protocol_error, invalid_message_in_hello}, State}.\n\n% In connected state, allow channel operations\nhandle_event({connected, _}, ?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data, State) ->\n    process_channel_request(Data, State).\n```\n\n## Fixing Authentication Bypass Vulnerabilities\n\n### Step 1: Identify Message Handlers\nFind all functions that handle SSH messages, especially:\n- Channel open\n- Channel request (exec, shell, subsystem)\n- Global requests\n\n### Step 2: Add Authentication Checks\nEnsure each handler verifies authentication state:\n\n```erlang\n% Pattern 1: Match on authenticated state\nhandle(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data, #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    process(Data, State);\nhandle(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, _, State) ->\n    {disconnect, not_authenticated, State}.\n\n% Pattern 2: Guard clause\nhandle(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data, State)\n    when State#state.authenticated =:= true ->\n    process(Data, State);\nhandle(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, _, State) ->\n    {disconnect, not_authenticated, State}.\n\n% Pattern 3: Explicit check\nhandle(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data, State) ->\n    case State#state.authenticated of\n        true -> process(Data, State);\n        false -> {disconnect, not_authenticated, State}\n    end.\n```\n\n### Step 3: Test Edge Cases\n- Message sent before version exchange\n- Message sent during key exchange\n- Message sent before authentication\n- Message sent after failed authentication\n- Message sent after successful authentication (should work)\n\n## References\n- RFC 4252: SSH Authentication Protocol\n- RFC 4254: SSH Connection Protocol\n- CVE patterns: Pre-authentication command Records\n```erlang\n-record(state, {\n    field1,\n    field2 = default_value,\n    field3 :: type()\n}).\n```\n\n### Macros\n```erlang\n-define(CONSTANT, 42).\n-define(SSH_MSG_DISCONNECT, 1).\n```\n\n## Finding Functions\n\n### Grep Patterns for Erlang\n\n```bash\n# Find function definitions\ngrep -n \"^function_name(\" file.erl\n\n# Find all exported functions\ngrep -n \"^-export\" State}.\nhandle_call(Request, From, State) -> {reply, Reply, NewState}.\nhandle_cast(Msg, State) -> {noreply, NewState}.\nhandle_info(Info, State) -> {noreply, NewState}.\n```\n\n### {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_014hVLJvgBzatyyfzvzzJN8V","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01XhmeasPgf96oW3FgFRiB2z","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/app/workspace/environment/skills/erlang-security-patching.md","content":"# Erlang Security Patching Best Practices\n\n## Overview\nWhen fixing security vulnerabilities in Erlang code, it's important to apply defense-in-depth principles and ensure the fix doesn't break existing functionality.\n\n## Security Fix Principles\n\n### 1. Fail Secure\nWhen in doubt, reject the operation:\n```erlang\n% GOOD - default to rejection\nprocess_request(Req, #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    allow(Req, State);\nprocess_request(_Req, State) ->\n    reject(State).  % Default deny\n\n% BAD - default to allowing\nprocess_request(Req, #state{authenticated = false} = State) ->\n    reject(State);\nprocess_request(Req, State) ->\n    allow(Req, State).  % Dangerous default!\n```\n\n### 2. Whitelist Over Blacklist\nExplicitly allow known-safe operations rather than blocking known-bad ones:\n```erlang\n% GOOD - whitelist allowed pre-auth messages\nallow_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_KEXINIT) -> true;\nallow_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_NEWKEYS) -> true;\nallow_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_SERVICE_REQUEST) -> true;\nallow_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_REQUEST) -> true;\nallow_pre_auth(_) -> false.\n\n% BAD - blacklist dangerous messages (easy to miss one)\nblock_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST) -> true;\nblock_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN) -> true;\nblock_pre_auth(_) -> false.  % Everything else allowed!\n```\n\n### 3. Defense in Depth\nAdd multiple layers of security checks:\n```erlang\n% Layer 1: State machine - only allow in connected state\nhandle_event({connected, _}, Msg, Data) ->\n    handle_connected_msg(Msg, Data);\nhandle_event(_State, ?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, _Data) ->\n    {keep_state_and_data, [{reply, error}]}.\n\n% Layer 2: Message handler - verify authentication\nhandle_connected_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{} = Msg,\n                     #data{authenticated = true} = Data) ->\n    process_channel_request(Msg, Data);\nhandle_connected_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{}, _Data) ->\n    {disconnect, not_authenticated}.\n\n% Layer 3: Channel operation - verify channel exists\nprocess_channel_request(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = Id},\n                        #data{channels = Channels} = Data) ->\n    case maps:find(Id, Channels) of\n        {ok, Channel} -> execute_on_channel(Channel, Data);\n        error -> {disconnect, invalid_channel}\n    end.\n```\n\n## Common Fix Patterns\n\n### Pattern 1: Add Authentication Guard\n```erlang\n% BEFORE\nhandle_msg(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data, State) ->\n    process(Data, State).\n\n% AFTER - Pattern match on authenticated state\nhandle_msg(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data,\n           #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    process(Data, State);\nhandle_msg(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, _Data, State) ->\n    {disconnect, ?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR, State}.\n```\n\n### Pattern 2: State-Based Dispatch\n```erlang\n% BEFORE\nhandle_message(Type, Data, State) ->\n    process_any_message(Type, Data, State).\n\n% AFTER - Separate pre-auth and post-auth handlers\nhandle_message(Type, Data, #state{authenticated = false} = State) ->\n    handle_pre_auth_message(Type, Data, State);\nhandle_message(Type, Data, #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    handle_post_auth_message(Type, Data, State).\n\nhandle_pre_auth_message(?SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_REQUEST, Data, State) ->\n    process_auth(Data, State);\nhandle_pre_auth_message(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, _Data, State) ->\n    {disconnect, ?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR, State}.\n```\n\n### Pattern 3: Message Type Validation\n```erlang\n% Add validation function\nis_pre_auth_allowed(?SSH_MSG_DISCONNECT) -> true;\nis_pre_auth_allowed(?SSH_MSG_IGNORE) -> true;\nis_pre_auth_allowed(?SSH_MSG_DEBUG) -> true;\nis_pre_auth_allowed(?SSH_MSG_KEXINIT) -> true;\nis_pre_auth_allowed(?SSH_MSG_NEWKEYS) -> true;\nis_pre_auth_allowed(N) when N >= 30, N =< 49 -> true; % KEX messages\nis_pre_auth_allowed(?SSH_MSG_SERVICE_REQUEST) -> true;\nis_pre_auth_allowed(?SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_REQUEST) -> true;\nis_pre_auth_allowed(_) -> false.\n\n% Use in dispatcher\ndispatch_message(Type, Data, #state{authenticated = false} = State) ->\n    case is_pre_auth_allowed(Type) of\n        true -> handle_message(Type, Data, State);\n        false -> {disconnect, ?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR, State}\n    end.\n```\n\n## Testing the Fix\n\n### Test Cases to Verify\n\n1. **Pre-auth rejection**: Send channel request before authentication\n   - Expected: Connection closed/rejected\n   - Verify: No command execution\n\n2. **Post-auth acceptance**: Complete authentication, then send channel request\n   - Expected: Command executes normally\n   - Verify: Normal SSH functionality\n\n3. **Partial auth**: Start authentication but don't complete\n   - Expected: Channel requests still rejected\n   - Verify: State tracking is correct\n\n4. **Normal operations**: Regular SSH sessions\n   - Expected: All normal operations work\n   - Verify: No regression in functionality\n\n### Example Test Scenarios\n```erlang\n% Test 1: Pre-auth channel request should fail\ntest_pre_auth_exec() ->\n    {ok, Sock} = gen_tcp:connect(\"localhost\", 22, [binary]),\n    ok = gen_tcp:send(Sock, version_string()),\n    % Skip authentication, send channel request directly\n    ok = gen_tcp:send(Sock, encode_channel_request(exec, \"whoami\")),\n    % Should receive disconnect or error, NOT command output\n    {ok, Response} = gen_tcp:recv(Sock, 0, 1000),\n    ?assert(is_disconnect(Response)).\n\n% Test 2: Post-auth should work\ntest_post_auth_exec() ->\n    {ok, Sock} = connect_and_authenticate(),\n    ok = gen_tcp:send(Sock, encode_channel_request(exec, \"whoami\")),\n    {ok, Response} = gen_tcp:recv(Sock, 0, 1000),\n    ?assert(is_success(Response)).\n```\n\n## Documentation\n\nWhen creating a security fix:\n1. Document the vulnerability clearly\n2. Explain the fix approach\n3. Note any behavior changes\n4. List test cases that verify the fix\n\n### Example Commit Message\n```\nFix pre-authentication command execution vulnerability\n\nVulnerability: The SSH server processed SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST\nmessages before user authentication was complete, allowing\nunauthenticated attackers to execute arbitrary commands.\n\nRoot cause: The handle_msg/3 function in ssh_connection.erl\ndid not verify the authentication state before processing\nchannel requests.\n\nFix: Added pattern match to only process SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST\nwhen State#state.authenticated =:= true. Pre-authentication\nchannel requests now result in SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR.\n\nTesting: Verified that:\n- Pre-auth channel requests are rejected\n- Post-auth channel requests work normally\n- Normal SSH operations are unaffected\n```\n\n## Avoiding Regressions\n\n### Check Existing Behavior\nBefore fixing:\n1. Understand what currently works\n2. Identify which code paths are used in normal operation\n3. Ensure fix only blocks unauthorized paths\n\n### Minimal Changes\n- Change only what's necessary\n- Don't refactor while fixing security issues\n- Keep fixes focused and reviewable\n\n### Backward Compatibility\n```erlang\n% If possible, maintain compatible behavior for authenticated users\nhandle(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data, #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    % Existing behavior unchanged\n    existing_handler(Data, State);\nhandle(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, _Data, State) ->\n    % New security check\n    {disconnect, protocol_error, State}.\n```\n\n## Common Pitfalls\n\n1. **Incomplete pattern matching**: Forgetting the catch-all clause\n2. **Wrong default**: Making the permissive clause the catch-all\n3. **State confusion**: Using wrong state field for authentication check\n4. **Timing issues**: TOCTOU (Time-Of-Check-Time-Of-Use) bugs\n5. **Multiple code paths**: Fixing one path but missing alternate routes\n\n## Review Checklist\n\nBefore submitting a security fix:\n- [ ] Does the fix block the vulnerable operation pre-auth?\n- [ ] Does the fix maintain normal functionality post-auth?\n- [ ] Are there multiple code paths? Are they all fixed?\n- [ ] Is the default behavior secure (fail closed)?\n- [ ] Are error messages non-informative to attackers?\n- [ ] Have you tested both positive and negative cases?\n- [ ] Is the fix minimal and focused?\n- [ ] Is the vulnerability properly documented?\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4272,"cache_read_input_tokens":19388,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":4272,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"67a03150-1814-4744-9310-d4e4323cf882","uuid":"992235cf-6463-4033-9199-4f1770890947"}

Erlang Code Navigation and Analysis\n\n## Overview\nNavigating large Erlang codebases requires understanding module structure, function exports, and inter-module dependencies.\n\n## Erlang File Structure\n\n### Module Declaration\n```erlang\n-module(module_name).  % Must match filename\n```\n\n### Exports\n```erlang\n-export([function1/1, function2/2]).  % Public functions\n```\n\n### Records\n```erlang\n-record(state, {\n    field1,\n    field2 = default_value,\n    field3 :: type()\n}).\n```\n\n### Macros\n```erlang\n-define(CONSTANT, 42).\n-define(SSH_MSG_DISCONNECT, 1).\n```\n\n## Finding Functions\n\n### Grep Patterns for Erlang\n\n```bash\n# Find function definitions\ngrep -n \"^function_name(\" file.erl\n\n# Find all exported functions\ngrep -n \"^-export\" file.erl\n\n# Find record definitions\ngrep -n \"^-record\" file.erl\n\n# Find macro definitions\ngrep -n \"^-define\" file.erl\n\n# Find message type constants\ngrep -n \"SSH_MSG_\" *.erl\n\n# Find function calls\ngrep -n \"module:function(\" file.erl\n```\n\n## Common Erlang/OTP Patterns\n\n### gen_server\n```erlang\n-behaviour(gen_server).\n\n% Callbacks\ninit(Args) -> {ok, State}.\nhandle_call(Request, From, State) -> {reply, Reply, NewState}.\nhandle_cast(Msg, State) -> {noreply, NewState}.\nhandle_info(Info, State) -> {noreply, NewState}.\n```\n\n### gen_statem\n```erlang\n-behaviour(gen_statem).\n\n% Callbacks\ncallback_mode() -> state_functions.  % or handle_event_function\n\n% State function mode\nstate_name(EventType, EventContent, Data) ->\n    {next_state, NextState, NewData}.\n\n% Handle event mode\nhandle_event(EventType, EventContent, State, Data) ->\n    {next_state, NextState, NewData}.\n```\n\n## SSH Module Structure\n\n### Key Modules in Erlang SSH\n- `ssh.erl`: Main API\n- `ssh_connection_handler.erl`: State machine for connections\n- `ssh_connection.erl`: Connection protocol implementation\n- `ssh_channel.erl`: Channel management\n- `ssh_auth.erl`: Authentication\n- `ssh_message.erl`: Message encoding/decoding\n- `ssh_transport.erl`: Transport layer\n\n### Message Flow\n1. Message arrives from network\n2. `ssh_transport.erl` decodes binary\n3. `ssh_connection_handler.erl` dispatches to appropriate handler\n4. Handler processes message and updates state\n\n## Searching for Vulnerabilities\n\n### Key Search Patterns\n\n```bash\n# Find message handlers\ngrep -rn \"SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST\" .\n\n# Find authentication checks\ngrep -rn \"authenticated\" . | grep -v \".beam\"\n\n# Find state record definitions\ngrep -rn \"^-record(state\" .\n\n# Find function clauses that might lack auth checks\ngrep -rn \"handle.*SSH_MSG\" .\n\n# Find channel request handling\ngrep -rn \"channel.*request\" . -i\n```\n\n## Understanding Data Flow\n\n### Tracing Message Handling\n1. Find message constant: `?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST`\n2. Search for pattern matching on that constant\n3. Trace function calls from handler\n4. Check if authentication is verified at any point\n\n### Example Analysis\n```erlang\n% Found in ssh_connection_handler.erl\nhandle_event(internal, #ssh_msg_channel_request{} = Msg, State, Data) ->\n    % Does this check authentication?\n    % Follow to see what it calls\n    ssh_connection:handle_msg(Msg, State, Data).\n\n% Follow to ssh_connection.erl\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{...}, State, Data) ->\n    % Check if State or Data contains auth info\n    % Check if there's an auth guard or pattern match\n    process_channel_request(...).\n```\n\n## Code Review Checklist\n\nFor security-critical functions, check:\n1. Is there a pattern match on `authenticated = true`?\n2. Is there a guard clause checking authentication?\n3. Is there an explicit if/case checking auth state?\n4. Can this code path be reached before authentication?\n5. Are there multiple function clauses? Which one matches first?\n\n## Tools and Techniques\n\n### Using grep effectively\n```bash\n# Find all message type definitions\ngrep -rn \"define(SSH_MSG\" . --include=\"*.hrl\"\n\n# Find all handlers for a specific message\ngrep -rn \"SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST\" . --include=\"*.erl\" -A 5\n\n# Find authentication state checks\ngrep -rn \"authenticated.*true\" . --include=\"*.erl\"\n```\n\n### Finding related files\n```bash\n# Find all SSH-related modules\nfind . -name \"ssh*.erl\"\n\n# Find header files with definitions\nfind . -name \"*.hrl\" | xargs grep \"SSH_MSG\"\n```\n\n## Common Vulnerability Locations\n\nIn SSH implementations, check:\n1. **Channel request handlers**: Do they verify auth?\n2. **Global request handlers**: Pre-auth allowed?\n3. **Service request handlers**: Which services allowed pre-auth?\n4. **State machine transitions**: Can states be skipped?\n5. **Message dispatch functions**: Default clauses that bypass checks?\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Fail Secure\nWhen in doubt, reject the operation:\n```erlang\n% GOOD - default to rejection\nprocess_request(Req, #state{authenticated = true} = State) ->\n    allow(Req, State);\nprocess_request(_Req, State) ->\n    reject(State).  % Default deny\n\n% BAD - default to allowing\nprocess_request(Req, #state{authenticated = false} = State) ->\n    reject(State);\nprocess_request(Req, State) ->\n    allow(Req, State).  % Dangerous default!\n```\n\n### 2. Whitelist Over Blacklist\nExplicitly allow known-safe operations rather than blocking known-bad ones:\n```erlang\n% GOOD - whitelist allowed pre-auth messages\nallow_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_KEXINIT) -> true;\nallow_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_NEWKEYS) -> true;\nallow_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_SERVICE_REQUEST) -> true;\nallow_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_REQUEST) -> true;\nallow_pre_auth(_) -> false.\n\n% BAD - blacklist dangerous messages (easy to miss one)\nblock_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST) -> true;\nblock_pre_auth(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN) -> true;\nblock_pre_auth(_) -> false.  % Everything else allowed!\n```\n\n### 3. Defense in Depth\nAdd multiple layers of security checks:\n```erlang\n% Layer 1: State machine - only allow in connected state\nhandle_event({connected, _}, Msg, Data) ->\n    handle_connected_msg(Msg, Data);\nhandle_event(_State, ?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, _Data) ->\n    {keep_state_and_data, [{reply, error}]}.\n\n% Layer 2: Message handler - verify authentication\nhandle_connected_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{} = Msg,\n                     #data{authenticated = true} = Data) ->\n    process_channel_request(Msg, Data);\nhandle_connected_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{}, _Data) ->\n    {disconnect, not_authenticated}.\n\n% Layer 3: Channel operation - verify channel exists\nprocess_channel_request(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = Id},\n                        #data{channels = Channels} = Data) ->\n    case maps:find(Id, Channels) of\n        {ok, Channel} -> execute_on_channel(Channel, Data);\n        error -> {disconnect, invalid_channel}\n    end.\n```\n\n## Common Fix Patterns\n\n### Pattern 1: Add Authentication Guard\n```erlang\n% BEFORE\nhandle_msg(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST, Data, State) ->\n    process(Data, State).\n\n% AFTER - and initialization helpers\ninit_connection_record(Role, Socket, Opts) ->\n    {WinSz, PktSz} = init_inet_buffers_window(Socket),\n    C = #connection{channel_cache = ssh_client_channel:cache_create(),\n                    channel_id_seed = 0,\n                    suggest_window_size = WinSz,\n                    suggest_packet_size = PktSz,\n                    requests = [],\n                    options = Opts,\n                    connection_supervisor = ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(connection_sup, Opts)\n                   },\n    case Role of\n        server ->\n            C#connection{cli_spec =\n                             ?GET_OPT(ssh_cli, Opts, {ssh_cli,[?GET_OPT(shell, Opts)]}),\n                         exec =\n                             ?GET_OPT(exec, Opts)};\n        client ->\n            C\n    end.\n\ninit_ssh_record(Role, Socket, Opts) ->\n    %% Export of this internal function is\n    %% intended for low-level protocol test suites\n    {ok,PeerAddr} = inet:peername(Socket),\n    init_ssh_record(Role, Socket, PeerAddr, Opts).\n\ninit_ssh_record(Role, Socket, PeerAddr, Opts) ->\n    AuthMethods = ?GET_OPT(auth_methods, Opts),\n    S0 = #ssh{role = Role,\n\t      opts = Opts,\n\t      userauth_supported_methods = AuthMethods,\n\t      available_host_keys = available_hkey_algorithms(Role, Opts),\n\t      random_length_padding = ?GET_OPT(max_random_length_padding, Opts)\n\t   },\n\n    {Vsn, Version} = ssh_transport:versions(Role, Opts),\n    LocalName = case inet:sockname(Socket) of\n                    {ok,Local} -> Local;\n                    _ -> undefined\n                end,\n    case Role of\n\tclient ->\n\t    PeerName = case ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(host, Opts, element(1,PeerAddr)) of\n                           PeerIP when is_tuple(PeerIP) ->\n                               inet_parse:ntoa(PeerIP);\n                           PeerName0 when is_atom(PeerName0) ->\n                               atom_to_list(PeerName0);\n                           PeerName0 when is_list(PeerName0) ->\n                               PeerName0\n                       end,\n            ssh_lib:set_label(client,\n                              {connection, list_to_binary(PeerName), Socket}),\n            S1 =\n                S0#ssh{c_vsn = Vsn,\n                       c_version = Version,\n                       opts = ?PUT_INTERNAL_OPT({io_cb, case ?GET_OPT(user_interaction, Opts) of\n                                                            true ->  ssh_io;\n                                                            false -> ssh_no_io\n                                                        end},\n                                                Opts),\n                       userauth_quiet_mode = ?GET_OPT(quiet_mode, Opts),\n                       peer = {PeerName, PeerAddr},\n                       local = LocalName\n                      },\n            S1#ssh{userauth_pubkeys = [K || K <- ?GET_OPT(pref_public_key_algs, Opts),\n                                            is_usable_user_pubkey(K, S1)\n                                      ]\n                  };\n\n\tserver ->\n            ssh_lib:set_label(server, {connection, Socket}),\n\t    S0#ssh{s_vsn = Vsn,\n\t\t   s_version = Version,\n\t\t   userauth_methods = string:tokens(AuthMethods, \",\"),\n\t\t   kb_tries_left = 3,\n\t\t   peer = {undefined, PeerAddr},\n                   local = LocalName\n\t\t  }\n    end.\n\nhandshake(ConnPid, server, Ref, Timeout) ->\n    receive\n\t{ConnPid, ssh_connected} ->\n\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n\t    {ok, ConnPid};\n\t{ConnPid, {not_connected, Reason}} ->\n\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n\t    {error, Reason};\n\t{'DOWN', Ref, process, ConnPid, {shutdown, Reason}} ->\n\t    {error, Reason};\n\t{'DOWN', Ref, process, ConnPid, Reason} ->\n\t    {error, Reason};\n        {'EXIT',_,Reason} ->\n            stop(ConnPid),\n            {error, {exit,Reason}}\n    after Timeout ->\n\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n\t    ssh_connection_handler:stop(ConnPid),\n\t    {error, timeout}\n    end;\nhandshake(ConnPid, client, Ref, Timeout) ->\n    receive\n\t{ConnPid, ssh_connected} ->\n\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n\t    {ok, ConnPid};\n\t{ConnPid, {not_connected, Reason}} ->\n\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n\t    {error, Reason};\n\t{'DOWN', Ref, process, ConnPid, {shutdown, Reason}} ->\n\t    {error, Reason};\n\t{'DOWN', Ref, process, ConnPid, Reason} ->\n\t    {error, Reason}\n    after Timeout ->\n\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n\t    ssh_connection_handler:stop(ConnPid),\n\t    {error, timeout}\n    end.\n\nhandshake(Msg, #data{starter = User}) ->\n    User ! {self(), Msg}.\n\n%%====================================================================\n%% gen_statem callbacks\n%%====================================================================\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n-type event_content() ::  any().\n\n-type renegotiate_flag() :: init | renegotiate.\n\n-type state_name() :: \n        {hello,                     role()                    }\n      | {kexinit,                   role(), renegotiate_flag()}\n      | {key_exchange,              role(), renegotiate_flag()}\n      | {key_exchange_dh_gex_init,  server, renegotiate_flag()}\n      | {key_exchange_dh_gex_reply, client, renegotiate_flag()}\n      | {new_keys,                  role(), renegotiate_flag()}\n      | {ext_info,                  role(), renegotiate_flag()}\n      | {service_request,           role()                    }\n      | {userauth,                  role()                    }\n      | {userauth_keyboard_interactive,       role()          }\n      | {userauth_keyboard_interactive_extra, server          }\n      | {userauth_keyboard_interactive_info_response, client  }\n      | {connected,                 role()                    }\n\t.\n\n%% The state names must fulfill some rules regarding\n%% where the role() and the renegotiate_flag() is placed:\n\n-spec renegotiation(state_name()) -> boolean().\nrenegotiation({_,_,ReNeg}) -> ReNeg == renegotiate;\nrenegotiation(_) -> false.\n\n\n-define(CONNECTED(StateName),\n        (element(1,StateName) == connected orelse\n         element(1,StateName) == ext_info ) ).\n\n-spec handle_event(gen_statem:event_type(),\n\t\t   event_content(),\n\t\t   state_name(),\n\t\t   #data{}\n\t\t  ) -> gen_statem:event_handler_result(state_name()) .\n\n-define(CONNECTION_MSG(Msg),\n        [{next_event, internal, prepare_next_packet},\n         {next_event,internal,{conn_msg,Msg}}]).\n\n%% . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .\ncallback_mode() ->\n    [handle_event_function,\n     state_enter].\n\n%%% ######## {hello, client|server} ####\n%% The very first event that is sent when ssh_connection_handler\n%% becomes owner process for Socket\nhandle_event(cast, socket_control, {wait_for_socket, Role},\n             #data{socket = Socket, ssh_params = #ssh{opts = Opts}}) ->\n    case inet:peername(Socket) of\n        {ok, PeerAddr} ->\n            try\n                {Protocol, Callback, CloseTag} = ?GET_OPT(transport, Opts),\n                D = #data{starter = ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(user_pid, Opts),\n                          socket = Socket,\n                          transport_protocol = Protocol,\n                          transport_cb = Callback,\n                          transport_close_tag = CloseTag,\n                          ssh_params = init_ssh_record(Role, Socket, PeerAddr, Opts),\n                          connection_state = init_connection_record(Role, Socket, Opts)\n                         },\n                NextEvent = {next_event, internal, socket_ready},\n                {next_state, {hello,Role}, D, NextEvent}\n            catch\n                _:{error,Error} -> {stop, {error,Error}};\n                error:Error ->     {stop, {error,Error}}\n            end;\n\n        {error,Error} ->\n            {stop, {shutdown,Error}}\n    end;\n\nhandle_event(internal, socket_ready, {hello,_}=StateName, #data{ssh_params = Ssh0} = D) ->\n    VsnMsg = ssh_transport:hello_version_msg(string_version(Ssh0)),\n    send_bytes(VsnMsg, D),\n    case inet:getopts(Socket=D#data.socket, [recbuf]) of\n\t{ok, [{recbuf,Size}]} ->\n\t    %% Set the socket to the hello text line handling mode:\n\t    inet:setopts(Socket, [{packet, line},\n\t\t\t\t  {active, once},\n\t\t\t\t  % Expecting the version string which might\n\t\t\t\t  % be max ?MAX_PROTO_VERSION bytes:\n\t\t\t\t  {recbuf, ?MAX_PROTO_VERSION},\n\t\t\t\t  {nodelay,true}]),\n            Time = ?GET_OPT(hello_timeout, Ssh0#ssh.opts, infinity),\n\t    {keep_state, D#data{inet_initial_recbuf_size=Size}, [{state_timeout,Time,no_hello_received}] };\n\n\tOther ->\n            ?call_disconnectfun_and_log_cond(\"Option return\", \n                                             io_lib:format(\"Unexpected getopts return:~n  ~p\",[Other]),\n                                             StateName, D),\n\t    {stop, {shutdown,{unexpected_getopts_return, Other}}}\n    end;\n\nhandle_event(internal, {info_line,_Line}, {hello,client}, D) ->\n    %% The server may send info lines to the client before the version_exchange\n    %% RFC4253/4.2\n    inet:setopts(D#data.socket, [{active, once}]),\n    keep_state_and_data;\n\nhandle_event(internal, {info_line,Line}, {hello,server}=StateName, D) ->\n    %% But the client may NOT send them to the server. Openssh answers with cleartext,\n    %% and so do we\n    send_bytes(\"Protocol mismatch.\", D),\n    Msg = io_lib:format(\"Protocol mismatch in version exchange. Client sent info lines.~n~s\",\n                        [ssh_dbg:hex_dump(Line, 64)]),\n    ?call_disconnectfun_and_log_cond(\"Protocol mismatch.\", Msg, StateName, D),\n    {stop, {shutdown,\"Protocol mismatch in version exchange. Client sent info lines.\"}};\n\nhandle_event(internal, {version_exchange,Version}, {hello,Role}, D0) ->\n    {NumVsn, StrVsn} = ssh_transport:handle_hello_version(Version),\n    case handle_version(NumVsn, StrVsn, D0#data.ssh_params) of\n\t{ok, Ssh1} ->\n\t    %% Since the hello part is finished correctly, we set the\n\t    %% socket to the packet handling mode (including recbuf size):\n\t    inet:setopts(D0#data.socket, [{packet,0},\n\t\t\t\t\t {mode,binary},\n\t\t\t\t\t {active, once},\n\t\t\t\t\t {recbuf, D0#data.inet_initial_recbuf_size}]),\n\t    {KeyInitMsg, SshPacket, Ssh} = ssh_transport:key_exchange_init_msg(Ssh1),\n\t    send_bytes(SshPacket, D0),\n            D = D0#data{ssh_params = Ssh,\n                        key_exchange_init_msg = KeyInitMsg},\n\t    {next_state, {kexinit,Role,init}, D, {change_callback_module, ssh_fsm_kexinit}};\n\n\tnot_supported ->\n            {Shutdown, D} =\n                ?send_disconnect(?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_VERSION_NOT_SUPPORTED,\n                                 io_lib:format(\"Offending version is ~p\",[string:chomp(Version)]),\n                                 {hello,Role},\n                                 D0),\n\t    {stop, Shutdown, D}\n    end;\n\n%%% timeout after tcp:connect but then nothing arrives\nhandle_event(state_timeout, no_hello_received, {hello,_Role}=StateName, D0 = #data{ssh_params = Ssh0}) ->\n    MsgFun =\n        fun (debug) ->\n                Time = ?GET_OPT(hello_timeout, Ssh0#ssh.opts),\n                lists:concat([\"No HELLO received within \",ssh_lib:format_time_ms(Time)]);\n            (_) ->\n                [\"No HELLO received within hello_timeout\"]\n        end,\n    {Shutdown, D} =\n        ?send_disconnect(?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR, ?SELECT_MSG(MsgFun), StateName, D0),\n    {stop, Shutdown, D};\n\n\n%%% ######## {service_request, client|server} ####\n\nhandle_event(internal, Msg = #ssh_msg_service_request{name=ServiceName}, StateName = {service_request,server}, D0) ->\n    case ServiceName of\n\t\"ssh-userauth\" ->\n\t    Ssh0 = #ssh{session_id=SessionId} = D0#data.ssh_params,\n\t    {ok, {Reply, Ssh}} = ssh_auth:handle_userauth_request(Msg, SessionId, Ssh0),\n            D = send_msg(Reply, D0#data{ssh_params = Ssh}),\n\t    {next_state, {userauth,server}, D, {change_callback_module,ssh_fsm_userauth_server}};\n\n\t_ ->\n            {Shutdown, D} =\n                ?send_disconnect(?SSH_DISCONNECT_SERVICE_NOT_AVAILABLE,\n                                 io_lib:format(\"Unknown service: ~p\",[ServiceName]),\n                                 StateName, D0),\n            {stop, Shutdown, D}\n    end;\n\nhandle_event(internal, #ssh_msg_service_accept{name = \"ssh-userauth\"}, {service_request,client},\n\t     #data{ssh_params = #ssh{service=\"ssh-userauth\"} = Ssh0} = D0) ->\n    {Msg, Ssh} = ssh_auth:init_userauth_request_msg(Ssh0),\n    D = send_msg(Msg, D0#data{ssh_params = Ssh,\n                              auth_user = Ssh#ssh.user\n                             }),\n    {next_state, {userauth,client}, D, {change_callback_module,ssh_fsm_userauth_client}};\n\n\n%% Skip ext_info messages in connected state (for example from OpenSSH >= 7.7)\nhandle_event(internal, #ssh_msg_ext_info{}, {connected,_Role}, D) ->\n    {keep_state, D};\n\nhandle_event(internal, {#ssh_msg_kexinit{},_}, {connected,Role}, D0) ->\n    {KeyInitMsg, SshPacket, Ssh} = ssh_transport:key_exchange_init_msg(D0#data.ssh_params),\n    D = D0#data{ssh_params = Ssh,\n\t\tkey_exchange_init_msg = KeyInitMsg},\n    send_bytes(SshPacket, D),\n    {next_state, {kexinit,Role,renegotiate}, D, [postpone, {change_callback_module,ssh_fsm_kexinit}]};\n\nhandle_event(internal, #ssh_msg_disconnect{description=Desc} = Msg, StateName, D0) ->\n    {disconnect, _, RepliesCon} =\n\tssh_connection:handle_msg(Msg, D0#data.connection_state, ?role(StateName), D0#data.ssh_params),\n    {Actions,D} = send_replies(RepliesCon, D0),\n    disconnect_fun(\"Received disconnect: \"++Desc, D),\n    {stop_and_reply, {shutdown,Desc}, Actions, D};\n\nhandle_event(internal, #ssh_msg_ignore{}, {_StateName, _Role, init},\n             #data{ssh_params = #ssh{kex_strict_negotiated = true,\n                                     send_sequence = SendSeq,\n                                     recv_sequence = RecvSeq}}) ->\n    ?DISCONNECT(?SSH_DISCONNECT_KEY_EXCHANGE_FAILED,\n                io_lib:format(\"strict KEX violation: unexpected SSH_MSG_IGNORE \"\n                              \"send_sequence = ~p  recv_sequence = ~p\",\n                              [SendSeq, RecvSeq])\n               );\n\nhandle_event(internal, #ssh_msg_ignore{}, _StateName, _) ->\n    keep_state_and_data;\n\nhandle_event(internal, #ssh_msg_unimplemented{}, _StateName, _) ->\n    keep_state_and_data;\n\n%% Quick fix of failing test case (ssh_options_SUITE:ssh_msg_debug_fun_option_{client|server}/1)\nhandle_event(cast, #ssh_msg_debug{} = Msg, State, D) ->\n    handle_event(internal, Msg, State, D);\n\nhandle_event(internal, #ssh_msg_debug{} = Msg, _StateName, D) ->\n    debug_fun(Msg, D),\n    keep_state_and_data;\n\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg,Msg}, StateName, #data{connection_state = Connection0,\n                                                        event_queue = Qev0} = D0) ->\n    Role = ?role(StateName),\n    Rengotation = renegotiation(StateName),\n    try ssh_connection:handle_msg(Msg, Connection0, Role, D0#data.ssh_params) of\n\t{disconnect, Reason0, RepliesConn} ->\n            {Repls, D} = send_replies(RepliesConn, D0),\n            case {Reason0,Role} of\n                {{_, Reason}, client} when ((StateName =/= {connected,client})\n                                            and (not Rengotation)) ->\n                    handshake({not_connected,Reason}, D);\n                _ ->\n                    ok\n            end,\n            {stop_and_reply, {shutdown,normal}, Repls, D};\n\n\t{Replies, Connection} when is_list(Replies) ->\n\t    {Repls, D} = \n\t\tcase StateName of\n\t\t    {connected,_} ->\n\t\t\tsend_replies(Replies, D0#data{connection_state=Connection});\n\t\t    _ ->\n\t\t\t{ConnReplies, NonConnReplies} = lists:splitwith(fun not_connected_filter/1, Replies),\n\t\t\tsend_replies(NonConnReplies, D0#data{event_queue = Qev0 ++ ConnReplies})\n\t\tend,\n            case {Msg, StateName} of\n                {#ssh_msg_channel_close{}, {connected,_}} ->\n                    {keep_state, D, [cond_set_idle_timer(D)|Repls]};\n                {#ssh_msg_channel_success{}, _} ->\n                    update_inet_buffers(D#data.socket),\n                    {keep_state, D, Repls};\n                _ ->\n                    {keep_state, D, Repls}\n            end\n\n    catch\n\tClass:Error ->\n            {Repls, D1} = send_replies(ssh_connection:handle_stop(Connection0), D0),\n            {Shutdown, D} = ?send_disconnect(?SSH_DISCONNECT_BY_APPLICATION,\n                                             io_lib:format(\"Internal error: ~p:~p\",[Class,Error]),\n                                             StateName, D1),\n            {stop_and_reply, Shutdown, Repls, D}\n    end;\n\n\nhandle_event(enter, OldState, {connected,_}=NewState, D) ->\n    %% Entering the state where re-negotiation is possible\n    init_renegotiate_timers(OldState, NewState, D);\n\nhandle_event(enter, OldState, {ext_info,_,renegotiate}=NewState, D) ->\n    %% Could be hanging in exit_info state if nothing else arrives\n    init_renegotiate_timers(OldState, NewState, D);\n\nhandle_event(enter, {connected,_}=OldState, NewState, D) ->\n    %% Exiting the state where re-negotiation is possible\n    pause_renegotiate_timers(OldState, NewState, D);\n\nhandle_event(cast, force_renegotiate, StateName, D) ->\n    handle_event({timeout,renegotiate}, undefined, StateName, D);\n\nhandle_event({timeout,renegotiate}, _, StateName, D0) ->\n    case StateName of\n        {connected,Role} ->\n            start_rekeying(Role, D0);\n        {ext_info,Role,renegotiate} ->\n            start_rekeying(Role, D0);\n        _ ->\n            %% Wrong state for starting a renegotiation, must be in re-negotiation\n            keep_state_and_data\n    end;\n\nhandle_event({timeout,check_data_size}, _, StateName, D0) ->\n    %% Rekey due to sent data limit reached? (Can't be in {ext_info,...} if data is sent)\n    case StateName of\n        {connected,Role} ->\n            check_data_rekeying(Role, D0);\n        _ ->\n            %% Wrong state for starting a renegotiation, must be in re-negotiation\n            keep_state_and_data\n    end;\n\nhandle_event({call,From}, get_alg, _, D) ->\n    #ssh{algorithms=Algs} = D#data.ssh_params,\n    {keep_state_and_data, [{reply,From,Algs}]};\n\nhandle_event(cast, _, StateName, _) when not ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    {keep_state_and_data, [postpone]};\n\nhandle_event(cast, {adjust_window,ChannelId,Bytes}, StateName, D) when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    case ssh_client_channel:cache_lookup(cache(D), ChannelId) of\n\t#channel{recv_window_size = WinSize,\n\t\t recv_window_pending = Pending,\n\t\t recv_packet_size = PktSize} = Channel\n\t  when (WinSize-Bytes) >= 2*PktSize ->\n\t    %% The peer can send at least two more *full* packet, no hurry.\n\t    ssh_client_channel:cache_update(cache(D),\n\t\t\t\t     Channel#channel{recv_window_pending = Pending + Bytes}),\n\t    keep_state_and_data;\n\n\t#channel{recv_window_size = WinSize,\n\t\t recv_window_pending = Pending,\n\t\t remote_id = Id} = Channel ->\n\t    %% Now we have to update the window - we can't receive so many more pkts\n\t    ssh_client_channel:cache_update(cache(D),\n\t\t\t\t     Channel#channel{recv_window_size =\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t WinSize + Bytes + Pending,\n\t\t\t\t\t\t     recv_window_pending = 0}),\n\t    Msg = ssh_connection:channel_adjust_window_msg(Id, Bytes + Pending),\n\t    {keep_state, send_msg(Msg,D)};\n\n\tundefined ->\n\t    keep_state_and_data\n    end;\n\nhandle_event(cast, {reply_request,Resp,ChannelId}, StateName, D) when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    case ssh_client_channel:cache_lookup(cache(D), ChannelId) of\n        #channel{remote_id = RemoteId} when Resp== success ; Resp==failure ->\n            Msg =\n                case Resp of\n                    success -> ssh_connection:channel_success_msg(RemoteId);\n                    failure -> ssh_connection:channel_failure_msg(RemoteId)\n                end,\n            update_inet_buffers(D#data.socket),\n            {keep_state, send_msg(Msg,D)};\n\n        #channel{} ->\n            Details = io_lib:format(\"Unhandled reply in state ~p:~n~p\", [StateName,Resp]),\n            {_Shutdown, D1} =\n                ?send_disconnect(?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR, Details, StateName, D),\n            {keep_state, D1};\n\n\tundefined ->\n\t    keep_state_and_data\n    end;\n\nhandle_event(cast, {request,ChannelPid, ChannelId, Type, Data}, StateName, D) when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    {keep_state,  handle_request(ChannelPid, ChannelId, Type, Data, false, none, D)};\n\nhandle_event(cast, {request,ChannelId,Type,Data}, StateName, D) when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    {keep_state,  handle_request(ChannelId, Type, Data, false, none, D)};\n\nhandle_event(cast, {unknown,Data}, StateName, D) when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    Msg = #ssh_msg_unimplemented{sequence = Data},\n    {keep_state, send_msg(Msg,D)};\n\nhandle_event(cast, {global_request, Type, Data}, StateName, D) when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    {keep_state, send_msg(ssh_connection:request_global_msg(Type,false,Data), D)};\n\n\n%%% Previously handle_sync_event began here\nhandle_event({call,From}, get_print_info, StateName, D) ->\n    Reply =\n\ttry\n\t    {inet:sockname(D#data.socket),\n\t     inet:peername(D#data.socket)\n\t    }\n\tof\n\t    {{ok,Local}, {ok,Remote}} ->\n\t\t{{Local,Remote},io_lib:format(\"statename=~p\",[StateName])};\n\t    _ ->\n\t\t{{\"-\",0},\"-\"}\n\tcatch\n\t    _:_ ->\n\t\t{{\"?\",0},\"?\"}\n\tend,\n    {keep_state_and_data, [{reply,From,Reply}]};\n\nhandle_event({call,From}, {connection_info, Options}, _, D) ->\n    Info = fold_keys(Options, fun conn_info/2, D),\n    {keep_state_and_data, [{reply,From,Info}]};\n\nhandle_event({call,From}, {channel_info,ChannelId,Options}, _, D) ->\n    case ssh_client_channel:cache_lookup(cache(D), ChannelId) of\n\t#channel{} = Channel ->\n\t    Info = fold_keys(Options, fun chann_info/2, Channel),\n\t    {keep_state_and_data, [{reply,From,Info}]};\n\tundefined ->\n\t    {keep_state_and_data, [{reply,From,[]}]}\n    end;\n\n\nhandle_event({call,From}, {info, all}, _, D) ->\n    Result = ssh_client_channel:cache_foldl(fun(Channel, Acc) ->\n\t\t\t\t\t     [Channel | Acc]\n\t\t\t\t     end,\n\t\t\t\t     [], cache(D)),\n    {keep_state_and_data, [{reply, From, {ok,Result}}]};\n\nhandle_event({call,From}, {info, ChannelPid}, _, D) ->\n    Result = ssh_client_channel:cache_foldl(\n\t       fun(Channel, Acc) when Channel#channel.user == ChannelPid ->\n\t\t       [Channel | Acc];\n\t\t  (_, Acc) ->\n\t\t       Acc\n\t       end, [], cache(D)),\n    {keep_state_and_data, [{reply, From, {ok,Result}}]};\n\nhandle_event({call,From}, {set_sock_opts,SocketOptions}, _StateName, D) ->\n    Result = try inet:setopts(D#data.socket, SocketOptions)\n             catch\n                 _:_ -> {error, badarg}\n             end,\n    {keep_state_and_data, [{reply,From,Result}]};\n\nhandle_event({call,From}, {get_sock_opts,SocketGetOptions}, _StateName, D) ->\n    Result = try inet:getopts(D#data.socket, SocketGetOptions)\n             catch\n                 _:_ -> {error, badarg}\n             end,\n    {keep_state_and_data, [{reply,From,Result}]};\n\nhandle_event({call,From}, stop, _StateName, D0) ->\n    {Repls,D} = send_replies(ssh_connection:handle_stop(D0#data.connection_state), D0),\n    {stop_and_reply, normal, [{reply,From,ok}|Repls], D};\n\nhandle_event({call,_}, _, StateName, _) when not ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    {keep_state_and_data, [postpone]};\n\nhandle_event({call,From}, {request, ChannelPid, ChannelId, Type, Data, Timeout}, StateName, D0)\n  when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    case handle_request(ChannelPid, ChannelId, Type, Data, true, From, D0) of\n        {error,Error} ->\n            {keep_state, D0, {reply,From,{error,Error}}};\n        D ->\n            %% Note reply to channel will happen later when reply is received from peer on the socket\n            start_channel_request_timer(ChannelId, From, Timeout),\n            {keep_state, D, cond_set_idle_timer(D)}\n    end;\n\nhandle_event({call,From}, {request, ChannelId, Type, Data, Timeout}, StateName, D0)\n  when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    case handle_request(ChannelId, Type, Data, true, From, D0) of\n        {error,Error} ->\n            {keep_state, D0, {reply,From,{error,Error}}};\n        D ->\n            %% Note reply to channel will happen later when reply is received from peer on the socket\n            start_channel_request_timer(ChannelId, From, Timeout),\n            {keep_state, D, cond_set_idle_timer(D)}\n    end;\n\nhandle_event({call,From}, {global_request, \"tcpip-forward\" = Type,\n                           {ListenHost,ListenPort,ConnectToHost,ConnectToPort},\n                           Timeout}, StateName, D0) when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    Id = make_ref(),\n    Data =  <<?STRING(ListenHost), ?Euint32(ListenPort)>>,\n    Fun = fun({success, <<Port:32/unsigned-integer>>}, C) ->\n                  Key = {tcpip_forward,ListenHost,Port},\n                  Value = {ConnectToHost,ConnectToPort},\n                  C#connection{options = ?PUT_INTERNAL_OPT({Key,Value}, C#connection.options)};\n             ({success, <<>>}, C) ->\n                  Key = {tcpip_forward,ListenHost,ListenPort},\n                  Value = {ConnectToHost,ConnectToPort},\n                  C#connection{options = ?PUT_INTERNAL_OPT({Key,Value}, C#connection.options)};\n             (_, C) ->\n                  C\n          end,\n    D = send_msg(ssh_connection:request_global_msg(Type, true, Data),\n                 add_request(Fun, Id, From, D0)),\n    start_channel_request_timer(Id, From, Timeout),\n    {keep_state, D, cond_set_idle_timer(D)};\n\nhandle_event({call,From}, {global_request, Type, Data, Timeout}, StateName, D0) when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    Id = make_ref(),\n    D = send_msg(ssh_connection:request_global_msg(Type, true, Data),\n                 add_request(true, Id, From, D0)),\n    start_channel_request_timer(Id, From, Timeout),\n    {keep_state, D, cond_set_idle_timer(D)};\n\nhandle_event({call,From}, {data, ChannelId, Type, Data, Timeout}, StateName, D0)\n  when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    {Repls,D} = send_replies(ssh_connection:channel_data(ChannelId, Type, Data, D0#data.connection_state, From),\n                             D0),\n    start_channel_request_timer(ChannelId, From, Timeout), % FIXME: No message exchange so why?\n    {keep_state, D, Repls};\n\nhandle_event({call,From}, {eof, ChannelId}, StateName, D0)\n  when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    case ssh_client_channel:cache_lookup(cache(D0), ChannelId) of\n\t#channel{remote_id = Id, sent_close = false} ->\n\t    D = send_msg(ssh_connection:channel_eof_msg(Id), #data{connection_state = #connection{options = Opts}} = D) when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    ConnectionSup = ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(connection_sup, Opts),\n    Reply = {ok, {ConnectionSup, ?role(StateName), Opts}},\n    {keep_state, D, [{reply,From,Reply}]};\n\nhandle_event({call,From},\n\t     {open, ChannelPid, Type, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Data, Timeout},\n\t     StateName,\n\t     D0 = #data{connection_state = C}) when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    erlang:monitor(process, ChannelPid),\n    {ChannelId, D1} = new_channel_id(D0),\n    WinSz = case InitialWindowSize of\n                undefined -> C#connection.suggest_window_size;\n                _ -> InitialWindowSize\n            end,\n    PktSz = case MaxPacketSize of\n                undefined -> C#connection.suggest_packet_size;\n                _ -> MaxPacketSize\n            end,\n    D2 = send_msg(ssh_connection:channel_open_msg(Type, ChannelId, WinSz, PktSz, Data),\n\t\t  D1),\n    ssh_client_channel:cache_update(cache(D2),\n\t\t\t     #channel{type = Type,\n\t\t\t\t      sys = \"none\",\n\t\t\t\t      user = ChannelPid,\n\t\t\t\t      local_id = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t start_channel_request_timer(ChannelId, From, Timeout),\n    {keep_state, D, cond_set_idle_timer(D)};\n\nhandle_event({call,From}, {send_window, ChannelId}, StateName, D)\n  when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    Reply = case ssh_client_channel:cache_lookup(cache(D), ChannelId) of\n\t\t#channel{send_window_size = WinSize,\n\t\t\t send_packet_size = Packsize} ->\n\t\t    {ok, {WinSize, ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    Reply = case ssh_client_channel:cache_lookup(cache(D), ChannelId) of\n\t\t#channel{recv_window_size = WinSize,\n\t\t\t recv_packet_size = Packsize} ->\n\t\t    {ok, {WinSize, Packsize}};\n\t\tundefined ->\n\t\t    {error, einval}\n\t    end,\n    {keep_state_and_data, [{reply,From,Reply}]};\n\nhandle_event({call,From}, {close, ChannelId}, StateName, D0)\n  when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    case ssh_client_channel:cache_lookup(cache(D0), ChannelId) of\n\t#channel{remote_id = Id} = Channel ->\n\t    D1 = send_msg(ssh_connection:channel_close_msg(Id), D0),\n\t [{reply,From,ok}]}\n    end;\n\nhandle_event(cast, {store,Key,Value}, _StateName, #data{connection_state=C0} = D) ->\n    C = C0#connection{options = ?PUT_INTERNAL_OPT({Key,Value}, C0#connection.options)},\n    {keep_state, D#data{connection_state = C}};\n\nhandle_event({call,From}, {retrieve,Key}, _StateName, #data{connection_state=C}) ->\n    case retrieve(C, Key) of\n        {ok,Value} ->\n            {keep_state_and_data, [{reply,From,{ok,Value}}]};\n        _ ->\n            {keep_state_and_data, [{reply,From,undefined}]}\n    end;\n\n%%===== Reception of encrypted bytes, decryption and framing\nhandle_event(info, {Proto, Sock, Info}, {hello,_}, #data{socket = Sock,\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t transport_protocol = Proto}) ->\n    case Info of\n\t\"SSH-\" ++ _ ->\n\t    {keep_state_and_data, [{next_event, internal, {version_exchange,Info}}]};\n\t_ ->\n\t    {keep_state_and_data, [{next_event, internal, {info_line,Info}}]}\n    end;\n\n\nhandle_event(info, {Proto, Sock, NewData}, StateName,\n             D0 = #data{socket = Sock,\n                        transport_protocol = Proto,\n                        ssh_params = SshParams}) ->\n    try D0#data.undecrypted_packet_length,\n\t  D0#data.ssh_params)\n    of\n\t{packet_decrypted, DecryptedBytes, EncryptedDataRest, Ssh1} ->\n\t    D1 = D0#data{ssh_params =\n\t\t\t    Ssh1#ssh{recv_sequence = ssh_transport:next_seqnum(Ssh1#ssh.recv_sequence)},\n\t\t\tdecrypted_data_buffer = <<>>,\n                        undecrypted_packet_length = undefined,\n                        aead_data =
